to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{j}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{q}^{q-1}}.

For the case in which
\mathcal{A}
is the
\ell_{2}
unit ball, we can use
Algorithm

2
with a covering argument using a finite
\mathcal{A}
of size
\Omega\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\epsilon^{-d}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
, which causes the regret in
Theorem

3.1
to scale as
d\sqrt{T\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}
. By our lower bound in
Theorem

4.1
, we know that this is suboptimal. Thus, in this section we consider a different algorithm that is designed for infinite action sets that include the standard basis vectors, such as
\ell_{p}
unit balls.

VALEE (
Algorithm

3
), is similar in spirit to the basic explore-exploit algorithm in classic MAB
(
25
, Algorithm 1.1)
, in the sense that it separates the exploration and exploitation stages.
However, our exploration is adaptive - it depends on the norm of the expected reward vector and its variance.
This simple approach yields an efficient and easy to implement algorithm that can achieve optimal regret (up to logarithmic factors) when the action set is an
\ell_{p}
unit ball for
p\in(1,2]
and the covariance matrix is known.

When the covariance matrix is known, VALEE operates in two phases - exploration and exploitation. The exploration phase plays only the standard basis vectors
e_{i}
, and uses the results to build an estimator for the expected reward vector
\theta^{\star}
. The estimation is done per coordinate using the median of means (MoM) method, in which we build
\kappa
independent estimators and use their median as the final estimator. This phase continues until our estimation error is small enough compared to the number of steps and variance of the rewards.

Next, the exploitation phase commits to an action
\hat{a}
, as defined in
Algorithm

3
, and plays it until the time is up. The reason for choosing this action is that the true optimal action
a^{\star}
is given by:

a^{\star}_{i}=\mathrm{sign}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}_{i}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}_{i}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{q-1}/\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{q}^{q-1},

and so
\hat{a}
is exactly
a^{\star}
with
\theta^{\star}
replaced by
\hat{\theta}^{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{j}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}
. If we think of
\hat{\theta}^{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{j}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}
as being a good estimator for
\theta^{\star}
, then
\hat{a}
is also a good estimator for
a^{\star}
. We state this formally in
Lemma

3.12
.

When the covariance matrix is unknown,
Algorithm

3
has an additional phase, preceding the exploration, for estimating the variance. In this phase, we use the
\mathcal{SR}
algorithm to estimate
\sigma^{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{e_{i}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
for all
i\in\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{d}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
and use these estimates for shortening the length of the exploration phase.

The regret bound of
Algorithm

3
when the covariance matrix is known, is given by the following theorem, proved in
Appendix

B
:

Theorem 3.7
.

Assume that the covariance matrix
\Sigma
is known. Then with probability
1-2\delta
, the regret of
Algorithm

3
on the
\ell_{p}
unit ball for
p\in(1,2]
and dual norm
q\geq 2
, is bounded by:

\widetilde{O}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{d+\sqrt{dTq\log\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{1/\delta}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\sigma_{q}^{2}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}.

Remark 3.8
.

This result matches our lower bound in
Theorem

4.1
up to logarithmic factors.

When the covariance matrix is unknown, the regret is as stated in the following theorem, proved in
Appendix

B
:

Theorem 3.9
.

Assume that the covariance matrix is
\Sigma
unknown. Then with probability
1-2\delta
the regret of
Algorithm

3
on the
\ell_{p}
unit ball for
p\in(1,2]
and dual norm
q\geq 2
, is bounded by:

\displaystyle\widetilde{O}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{d^{2/3+2/3q}\log^{2/3}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{1/\delta}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{Tq}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{1/3}+\sqrt{dTq\log\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{1/\delta}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\sigma_{q}^{2}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}.

3.2.1
Analysis and proof sketch

To provide intuition to our algorithm, we focus on the
\ell_{2}
unit ball.
The main idea in our analysis is leveraging the parameter noise model to show that exploring each standard basis vector for
1/\varepsilon^{2}
samples yields an estimation error of
\varepsilon\sqrt{\sigma^{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{e_{i}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}
. Previous work in the additive noise model showed that the same number of samples yields an error of
\varepsilon
, which is always worse than our error bound. Formally, this is stated in the following lemma, proved for the case of
p\in(1,2]
in
Lemma

B.9
:

Lemma 3.10
.

With probability
1-\delta
, for all
i\in\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{d}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
and
j\leq\log\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{T}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
jointly, we have that:

e_{i}^{\top}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\hat{\theta}^{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{j}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}-\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\leq\sqrt{\sigma^{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{e_{i}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}\hat{\varepsilon}_{j}
.

Equipped with this improved concentration bound, we leverage the geometry of the action set and Cauchy-Schwartz to show that we can get a
2
-norm estimation error of
\varepsilon\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
, whereas in the additive noise the same argument would result in an error of
\varepsilon\sqrt{d}
. This is stated in the following lemma, proved for the case of
p\in(1,2]
in
Lemma

B.10
:

Lemma 3.11
.

With probability
1-\delta
, for all
j\leq\log\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{T}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
jointly, we have that:

\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\hat{\theta}^{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{j}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}-\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}\leq\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}\hat{\varepsilon}_{j}
.

In
Lemma

B.3
we show that
\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\leq 1
. Moreover, when the variances are small,
\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
can be much smaller, leading to a significantly improved error bound.

Next, we prove that the
\ell_{2}
error between
\hat{a}
(the action that
Algorithm

3
commits to) and the optimal action, is controlled by the approximation error of
\hat{\theta}^{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{j}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}
. Intuitively, this is true since the
\ell_{2}
unit ball is curved and has a smooth surface, and so slightly changing the reward vector implies a slight a change in the optimal action. This is in contrast to, e.g., the simplex, which represents MAB, that has a flat surface. More formally, we prove the following lemma, proved for the case of
p\in(1,2]
in
Lemma

B.13
:

Lemma 3.12
.

If
Algorithm

3
reached the exploit stage after
L
iterations, then with probability
1-\delta
we have:

\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\hat{a}-a^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}\leq\frac{3\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}\hat{\varepsilon}_{L}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}}.

We use
Lemma

3.12
to upper bound the sub-optimality of
\hat{a}
, which determines the regret of the exploit phase. This is stated in the following lemma, proved for the case of
p\in(1,2]
in
Lemma

B.14
:

Lemma 3.13
.

If
Algorithm

3
reached the exploit stage after
L
iterations, then with probability
1-\delta
, the suboptimality gap of
\hat{a}
is bounded by:

\Delta_{\hat{a}}\leq\frac{3\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\hat{\varepsilon}_{L}^{2}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}}.

This error bound improves the standard concentration analysis which shows that the same amount of samples yields
\varepsilon
error. The reason for the improvement in our result is the linear structure of the reward function and the geometry of the action set, which has some curvature when
p=2
.

Using
Lemma

3.13
, ignoring constants and logarithmic factors, a simple analysis shows that the regret is bounded by:

d\varepsilon^{2}/\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}+T\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\varepsilon^{2}/\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}.

If we know
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}
but don’t know
\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
, choosing
\varepsilon\approx\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}/T}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{1/4}
yields a regret bound of
(1+\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}})\sqrt{dT}=O\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\sqrt{dT}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
.

However, assuming we know
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}
is not realistic, and so the initial phase of
Algorithm

3
is devoted to estimating the norm using a doubling argument.
We start by “guessing” that the norm is
1
, and perform the number of exploration steps required when this is true.
Then, we build an estimator
\hat{\theta}^{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{j}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}
and use its norm as an estimate for
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}
.
We stop doubling when the estimator’s norm is small compared to the number of exploration steps, and then continue to the exploitation stage.
We show that the we can approximate
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}
in the following lemma, proved for the case of
p\in(1,2]
in
Lemma

B.11
:

Lemma 3.14
.

If the exploration stage of
Algorithm

3
stops in iteration
L
, then with probability
1-\delta
we have that:

\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}/4\leq\hat{N}_{L}\leq 5\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}/2.

Using this result, we can show that we can get a regret bound of
O\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\sqrt{dT}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
, even without knowledge of
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}
.

Thus far, we were able to achieve a regret bound matching that of the adversarial case. Next, we will leverage the variance information in order to improve this bound. Building up to our actual algorithm, we first assume we know
\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
, and so can choose
\varepsilon
more subtly to depend on
\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
. Choosing
\varepsilon\approx\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{2}/T\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{1/4}
yields a variance dependent regret bound of
O\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{d+\sqrt{dT\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
, which might be much smaller than
O\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\sqrt{dT}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
when the variance is small. In fact, when the covariance matrix is known, this bound is optimal up to logarithmic factors, as we later show a matching lower bound (
Theorem

4.1
).

Finally, dropping the assumption that we know
\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
, we use a simple algorithm (
Algorithm

1
), to estimate it in order to tune
\varepsilon
. We show that we can get an estimation for
\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
, with an additive error that depends on a threshold
\tau\approx dT^{-1/3}
, stated precisely in
Appendix

B
. For the case of
p=q=2
, the lemma is as follows, and we prove it for the case of
p\in(1,2]
in
Lemma

B.7
:

Lemma 3.15
.

With probability
1-\delta
, the following hold jointly:

•

The variance estimation is good:

\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}/4\leq\hat{\sigma}_{q}^{2}\leq 3d\tau/2+3\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}/2.

•

The regret of the variance estimation is bounded by:

180d\log\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{2d/\delta}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}/\tau
.

As stated in
•
‣
Lemma

3.15
and by the choice of
\tau
, the variance estimation phase incurs an additive regret penalty of
dT^{1/3}
(
Theorem

3.9
).
This bound is still always better than the standard
\sqrt{dT}
regret bound, but when the variance is extremely small (smaller than
T^{-1/3}
), one might be able to get a better regret bound.
We leave closing this gap to future work, as noted in
Section

5
.

4
Lower Bounds

In this section, we present our lower bounds for the parameter noise setting, which depend on the variance of the reward distribution. We state three lower bounds - one for the case in which the action set is the
\ell_{p}
unit ball for
p\leq 2
, and two for the case where
p>2
. We also provide short proof sketches for both bounds. The full proofs can be seen in
Appendix

C
.

While
4
show lower bounds of
\sqrt{dT}
for
\ell_{p}
unit balls with
p\in(1,2]
and
d\sqrt{T}
for
p>2
in the adversarial setting, we present a more refined version that depends on
\sigma_{q}^{2}
- a measure of the reward variance. If we plug in a distribution with constant
\sigma_{q}^{2}
into our lower bound, we get lower bounds for the parameter noise model of
\sqrt{dT}
and
d\sqrt{T}
for
p\leq 2
and
p>2
, respectively. Since the parameter noise model is a relaxation of the adversarial model, this also implies the same lower bound for the adversarial setting. This is the exact proof methodology that
4
chose, by designing a stochastic adversary that samples reward vectors from a fixed distribution with constant
\sigma_{q}^{2}
. Thus, our result, which depends on
\sigma_{q}^{2}
, can be seen as a generalization of their result to the case of a general covariance matrix.

Formally, our lower bounds are stated in the following theorems:

Theorem 4.1
.

For any bandit algorithm
\mathcal{ALG}
running on the
\ell_{p}
unit ball for
p\in(1,2]
and dual norm
q\geq 2
,
T\geq d
and any
\sigma^{2}>0
, there exists a reward distribution
\mathcal{D}
with
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Sigma_{ii}^{q/2}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{2/q}=\sigma^{2}
such that
\mathcal{R}_{T}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\mathcal{ALG},D}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}=\widetilde{\Omega}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\sqrt{dT\sigma^{2}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
.

Remark 4.2
.

Theorem

4.1
shows that our result from
Theorem

3.7
is optimal, up to logarithmic factors.

Theorem 4.3
.

For any bandit algorithm
\mathcal{ALG}
running on the
\ell_{p}
unit ball for
p>2
and dual norm
q\in[1,2)
,
T\geq d^{\frac{4}{2-q}}
and any
\sigma^{2}>0
, there exists a reward distribution
\mathcal{D}
with
\sigma_{q}^{2}=\sigma^{2}
such that
\mathcal{R}_{T}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\mathcal{ALG},D}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}=\widetilde{\Omega}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{d\sqrt{T\sigma^{2}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
.

We note that
Theorem

4.3
can also be stated as follows, in terms of the maximal variance:

Theorem 4.4
.

For any bandit algorithm
\mathcal{ALG}
running on the
\ell_{p}
unit ball for
p>2
and dual norm
q\in[1,2)
,
T\geq d^{\frac{4}{2-q}}
and any
\sigma^{2}>0
, there exists a reward distribution
\mathcal{D}
with
\sigma_{\max}^{2}=\sigma^{2}/2
such that
\mathcal{R}_{T}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\mathcal{ALG},D}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}=\widetilde{\Omega}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{d\sqrt{T\sigma^{2}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
.

Our construction and proof of
Theorem

4.1
follow a similar method to that presented in
23
, by randomly choosing a vector
\xi\in\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{-1,1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{d}
, and choosing
\mathcal{D}
to be a Gaussian distribution centered around
\varepsilon\xi
for some small
\varepsilon
. Carefully choosing the covariance matrix as the scalar matrix with the largest possible variance in each direction, and setting
\varepsilon=d^{1/2-1/q}\sigma/\sqrt{T}
, yields our lower bound.

This shows that the regret of
Algorithm

3
, stated in
Theorem

3.7
, is optimal up to logarithmic factors, when the covariance matrix is known.

For
Theorem

4.3
, we adapt the proof technique and construction of
4
to our model. We choose a Gaussian distribution
\mathcal{D}
over
\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
, whose expectation is
(1,\varepsilon\xi)
, where
\xi
is again sampled uniformly from
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{-1,1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{d}
. The covariance matrix is chosen such that the variance of the first coordinate is roughly equal to that of the other coordinates combined. The intuition, as described by
4
, is that the learner must choose between exploration and exploitation rounds. In exploration rounds, the learner tries to learn the sign of
\xi
, whereas in exploitation rounds they place constant weight on the first coordinate.

4
then show that in order to achieve low regret, the learner must find the sign of
\xi
, and so exploitation is not sufficient. Since exploring, i.e. choosing a small value in the first coordinate, also incurs large regret, the learner must balance exploration and exploitation. Following their argument and carefully choosing
\varepsilon
such that
\varepsilon\approx\sigma/\sqrt{T}
yields our regret lower bound.

5
Discussion

In this work, we focused on the stochastic linear bandits with parameter noise model, and showed that for some action sets, we can get better regret bounds compared to the adversarial model.

We also presented novel lower bounds that generalize previous bounds to the case of a general covariance matrix of the reward distribution. This sheds more light on the optimal achievable regret in the parameter noise model.

While our results for the
\ell_{p}
unit ball with
p\leq 2
are optimal (up to logarithmic factors) in the case of a known covariance matrix, we incur an additive
dT^{1/3}
term when the covariance matrix is unknown. While we believe this can’t be mitigated with an explore-exploit algorithm, finding an algorithm which is optimal when the covariance matrix is unknown is left to future work.

Additionally, for
\ell_{p}
unit balls with
p>2
, we present the lower bound of
d\sqrt{T\sigma_{q}^{2}}
, and a natural and interesting open question is to find an algorithm whose regret bound matches this bound.

Appendix A
Regret Bound Proof for VASE

In this section, we prove the regret bound for
Algorithm

2
, as stated in
Theorem

3.1
. We start by setting some notation and proving some helpful lemmas.

A.1
Notations

Throughout our proof, we use the following notation:

\hat{B}_{\ell}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}}\hat{\sigma}^{2}_{\ell}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\pi_{\ell}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}},\quad\quad B_{\ell}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}}\sigma^{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\pi_{\ell}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}

We also denote enote by
M
the number of phases performed by the algorithm, and denote
T_{\ell}^{\mathcal{SR}}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}}T_{\ell}^{\mathcal{SR}}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
.

A.2
Proof

We start by proving a few lemmas.

Lemma A.1
.

B_{\ell}=\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma V\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\pi_{\ell}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}

Proof.

By the linearity and cyclic invariance of the trace, we have:

\displaystyle B_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}}\sigma^{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\pi_{\ell}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}}{a^{\top}\Sigma a\cdot\pi_{\ell}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}}\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma\pi_{\ell}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}aa^{\top}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}}\pi_{\ell}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}aa^{\top}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}=\mathrm{tr}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\Sigma V\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\pi_{\ell}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}

Where the first equality is by the definition of
\sigma^{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

for all
\ell
we have that:

\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{V_{\ell}^{-1}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{\ell}}{7\sqrt{\log\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{1/\delta_{\ell}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}}

Proof.

By our choice of
T_{\ell}
and since
g(\pi)\leq d

(
13
)
, we have:

\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{V_{\ell}^{-1}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{\ell}}{7\sqrt{d\log\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{1/\delta_{\ell}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}}\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rVert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{V\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\pi_{\ell}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{-1}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{\ell}}{7\sqrt{\log\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{1/\delta_{\ell}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}}

∎

Definition A.3
.

Define
\mathcal{E}_{var}
as the event in which the following inequalities hold for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and
a\in\mathrm{Supp}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\pi_{\ell}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
jointly:

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}^{2}_{\ell}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\geq\frac{1}{4}\max\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\varepsilon_{\ell},\sigma^{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}

(1)

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}^{2}_{\ell}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\leq\frac{3}{4}\sigma^{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}+\frac{3}{4}\varepsilon_{\ell}

(2)

.

\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\lvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\lvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\lvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\lvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a^{\top}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\hat{\theta}^{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\ell}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}-\theta^{\star}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\rvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\rvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\rvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\rvert\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\leq\varepsilon_{\ell}

.

Definition A.6
.

\mathcal{E}_{var}\cap\mathcal{E}_{lss}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{SR}steps}

Lemma A.7
.

When running
Algorithm

1
in phase
\ell
of
Algorithm

2
, the following inequalities hold for all
a\in\mathrm{Supp}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\pi_{\ell}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{Z_{s}(a)}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\max\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\varepsilon_{\ell},\sigma^{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}

(3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{Z_{s}(a)}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\ell}

(4)

Proof.

We first note that clearly
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{Z_{s}(a)}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}\geq\varepsilon_{\ell}/2
. Also, since
\theta_{s}
and
\theta_{s+1}
are sampled i.i.d:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{Z_{s}(a)}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\frac{1}{4}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a^{\top}\theta_{s}-a^{\top}\theta_{s+1}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{2}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}=\frac{1}{4}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{\mathbb{E}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a^{\top}\theta_{s}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}-2\mathbb{E}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a^{\top}\theta_{s}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a^{\top}\theta_{s+1}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox \displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a^{\top}\theta_{s}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{a^{\top}\theta_{s}}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox . Similarly:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left)\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\ell}

and the lemma.
